- Active motifs: milking with the fingertips (`quranic:root_001165:B005/m01`); a vessel in which drink is kept for days until it improves (`quranic:root_000067:B010/m01`); milk, honey, or another liquid thickening at its outcome (`quranic:root_000067:B005/m01`); a thick mixture of honey or milk (`quranic:root_000906:B015/m01`); reducing a drink until one third remains (`quranic:root_000203:B008/m01`); diluting a strong drink with water (`quranic:root_001200:B007/m01`); tasting and consuming (`quranic:root_000934:B001/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:11 `أُو۟لِى`; 73:13 `طَعَامًا`; 73:18 `مُنفَطِرٌ`; 73:20 `ثُلُثَهُ/يَضْرِبُونَ/يُقَٰتِلُونَ`
- Synthesis: Collection, waiting, thickening, blending, reduction, dilution, and tasting form a controlled transformation. This usefully reframes measured time and deferred outcome as processes that can mature nourishment, while sharply contrasting the surah’s choking food, whose material transformation defeats intake.

### S10. Whole Surah - Mountain Refuge for a Wild Herd
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Wild animals enter mountain terrain, where a returning ibex, its mother-and-young pair, a gathered herd, and a neighboring hyena occupy a protected refuge.
- Active motifs: entering mountain country (`quranic:root_000217:B006/m01`); a male ibex or stag returning to mountain shelter (`quranic:root_000067:B009/m01`); an ibex mother and young (`quranic:root_001096:B005/m01`); a herd of wild cattle or camels (`quranic:root_000532:B014/m01`); a male hyena (`quranic:root_001040:B007/m01`); a protective barrier around vulnerable life (`quranic:root_001677:B001/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:11 `أُو۟لِى`; 73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:17 `تَتَّقُونَ`; 73:19-20 `رَبِّهِ/رَبَّكَ`; 73:20 `عَلِمَ/غَفُورٌ`
- Synthesis: Terrain, entry, sheltering adult, mother and young, herd, neighboring animal, and barrier make the mountain a lived refuge rather than inert mass. This materially raises the stakes of 73:14: when mountains become poured sand, an ecological shelter and nursery also disappear.

### S11. Whole Surah - Receiving and Feeding a Stranger
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A road traveler enters a community not their own, is greeted, lodged in a fitting place, fed, and treated generously.
- Active motifs: an outsider entering a people to whom he does not belong (`quranic:root_000009:B006/m01`); road users and travelers (`quranic:root_000672:B002/m01`); conveying a formal greeting (`quranic:root_001211:B006/m01`); a place agreeing with its guest and making lodging pleasant (`quranic:root_001525:B011/m01`); feeding another in response to need (`quranic:root_000934:B002/m01`); generosity and donation (`quranic:root_000452:B005/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:11 `ٱلنَّعْمَةِ`; 73:13 `طَعَامًا`; 73:19-20 `سَبِيلًا/سَبِيلِ`; 73:20 `ءَاتُوا/ٱقْرَءُوا/خَيْرٍ`
- Synthesis: Arrival establishes strangerhood, greeting opens relation, lodging supplies safety, and food and gift complete reception. This materializes the surah’s travelers, giving, and mercy as hospitality to a mobile outsider rather than value transfer in the abstract.

### S12. Whole Surah - Crooked Setting of a Broken Bone
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hard bone or thick limb is broken, set, and healed on a bend that leaves the body visibly altered.
- Active motifs: solid bone (`quranic:root_001029:B005/m01`); the thick part of a limb (`quranic:root_001029:B003/m01`); bodily and skeletal thickness (`quranic:root_000217:B003/m01`); setting a fracture that heals crooked (`quranic:root_000015:B002/m01`); a disfigured form or face (`quranic:root_000832:B002/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:19 `شَآءَ`; 73:20 `أَعْظَمَ/أَجْرًا`
- Synthesis: Bone, limb, break, setting, healing, and residual deformation form a complete repair scene. The fracture-setting activation of `أَجْرًا` usefully reframes reward as restorative work, while crooked healing presses the surah’s concern with measured uprightness: repair must restore alignment, not merely close a break.

### S13. Whole Surah - Preparing a Bed and Lying Supine
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A thick wool rug is spread and softened into bedding, after which the occupant relaxes and lies on the back.
- Active motifs: a thick woolen rug (`quranic:root_000025:B005/m01`); smoothing a bed or seat until it is soft and easy (`quranic:root_001659:B002/m01`); ease, familiarity, and relaxed confidence (`quranic:root_000563:B007/m01`); lying flat on the back (`quranic:root_001372:B009/m01`); bodily standing (`quranic:root_001273:B002/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:2,6,20 `قُمِ/أَقْوَمُ/تَقُومُ`; 73:5 `نُلْقِى`; 73:6 `وَطْـًٔا`; 73:14,20 `ٱلْأَرْضُ/أَرْضِ`; 73:15-16 `أَرْسَلْنَا/ٱلرَّسُولَ`
- Synthesis: Rug, preparation, bodily ease, reclining, and standing create a precise posture contrast. The scene materializes the rest interrupted by the command to rise at night, making nocturnal discipline a bodily refusal of a fully prepared place of repose.

### S14. Whole Surah - Separating and Grafting a Palm
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A palm offshoot is separated, a young plant receives material from another tree, and successful union is tested against continued greenness or drying.
- Active motifs: a palm offshoot separated from its mother (`quranic:root_000082:B004/m01`); young or short palms (`quranic:root_000248:B006/m01`); inserting a branch from another tree and its accepting the union (`quranic:root_000934:B010/m01`); a plant that remains green (`quranic:root_000532:B012/m01`); a dried tree (`quranic:root_000217:B008/m01`); organic growth and increase (`quranic:root_000637:B001/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:8 `تَبَتَّلْ`; 73:13 `طَعَامًا`; 73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:17 `يَجْعَلُ`; 73:19-20 `رَبِّهِ/رَبَّكَ`; 73:20 `زَّكَوٰةَ`
- Synthesis: Separation, young stock, graft insertion, acceptance, growth, and drying form a propagation scene with a success condition. It usefully pressures `تَبَتَّلْ`: severance need not mean sterile isolation, because a cut relation can prepare a viable new attachment whose truth is shown by continued growth.

### S15. Whole Surah - Task Fee Weighed in an Active Market
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A task is commissioned for a stated fee, the market supplies exchange, and payment is weighed and delivered as wage.
- Active motifs: a market becoming active and saleable (`quranic:root_001273:B018/m01`); a fee or bounty assigned to a task (`quranic:root_000248:B005/m01`); the ounce as a known weight for dirhams or oil (`quranic:root_001677:B004/m01`); wage or reward for work (`quranic:root_000015:B001/m01`); silver, gold, or a precious vessel (`quranic:root_001077:B018/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:2,6,20 `قُمِ/أَقْوَمُ/تَقُومُ`; 73:9 `ٱلْمَغْرِبِ`; 73:17 `يَجْعَلُ/تَتَّقُونَ`; 73:20 `أَجْرًا`
- Synthesis: Commission, market, standard weight, payment material, and wage make a complete piecework transaction. This materializes the surah’s `أَجْرًا` as measured compensation while clarifying, by contrast, that the good loan and almsgiving exceed an ordinary fee-for-service exchange.

### S16. Whole Surah - Sworn and Documented Right
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A right is written, secured by covenant and surety, attested by a witness, and defended against repeated diminution.
- Active motifs: a written deed or document of right (`quranic:root_000516:B008/m01`); oath and sworn undertaking (`quranic:root_000076:B007/m01`); knowledgeable testimony (`quranic:root_000822:B002/m01`); covenant and protected agreement (`quranic:root_000532:B011/m01`); surety for another (`quranic:root_001332:B003/m01`); repeated subtraction from another’s right (`quranic:root_001542:B005/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:3 `ٱنقُصْ`; 73:8,19 `ٱذْكُرِ/تَذْكِرَةٌ`; 73:15 `شَٰهِدًا`; 73:14,18,20 `كَانَتِ/كَانَ/يَكُونُ`; 73:19-20 `رَبِّهِ/رَبَّكَ`; surface anchor unavailable for `quranic:root_000076:B007` because `ء ل ي` is missing from root coverage
- Synthesis: Record, oath, covenant, guarantor, witness, and threatened diminution fill the roles of a legal assurance scene. It materially supports the surah’s promise, testimony, loan, and accountability by showing how a future obligation is made durable and how partial erosion can still violate the whole right.

### S17. Whole Surah - Evil Eye and Binding Incantation
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A hostile gaze is perceived to strike a person, visible eye symptoms appear, and an incantation or charm is used to seize or restrain the affliction.
- Active motifs: injury by the evil eye (`quranic:root_001533:B003/m01`); an incantation believed to seize the eye (`quranic:root_000018:B004/m01`); an inflamed or jutting eye (`quranic:root_000225:B003/m01`); an eye-film like woven threads (`quranic:root_000672:B010/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:9,16,19 `فَٱتَّخِذْهُ/فَأَخَذْنَٰهُ/ٱتَّخَذَ`; 73:12 `جَحِيمًا`; 73:19-20 `سَبِيلًا/سَبِيلِ`; 73:20 `أَنفُسِكُم`
- Synthesis: Gaze, struck person, eye symptom, obscuring film, and binding rite form a complete culturally legible affliction scene. It usefully pressures the surah’s seizure and protection language by materializing how harm may be imagined as entering through perception and being answered by an act of restraint.

### S18. Whole Surah - Courtship, Verification, and Blocked Marriage
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A widowed or divorced woman receives suitors, courtship proceeds through message and self-presentation, her status is verified by waiting, and marriage may advance to consummation or be blocked by a charm.
- Active motifs: a widowed or divorced woman approached by suitors (`quranic:root_000563:B009/m01`); self-presentation or entreaty in courtship (`quranic:root_001200:B004/m01`); seclusion until menstruation verifies status (`quranic:root_001210:B010/m01`); a charm believed to restrain marriage or judgment (`quranic:root_000018:B004/m02`); the night of consummation (`quranic:root_000833:B003/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:4,20 `ٱلْقُرْءَانَ/ٱقْرَءُوا`; 73:5,16,19 `نُلْقِى/فَأَخَذْنَٰهُ/ٱتَّخَذَ`; 73:15-16 `أَرْسَلْنَا/رَسُولًا/ٱلرَّسُولَ`; 73:17 `شِيبًا`; 73:20 `يُقَٰتِلُونَ`
- Synthesis: Separation, renewed approach, message, waiting test, blocking device, and consummation define a regulated relational transition. The scene usefully reframes the surah’s severance and delay by showing that social withdrawal can initiate a timed reordering of attachment rather than simple abandonment.

### S19. Whole Surah - Pasture Greening, Grazing, and Abandonment
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A gentle wind moves over persistent greenery, herd animals graze a named forage, and the vegetation eventually dries, breaks, and is left behind.
- Active motifs: a gentle moist south wind (`quranic:root_001525:B009/m01`); a plant that remains green (`quranic:root_000532:B012/m01`); camel forage called `الصِّليان` (`quranic:root_000879:B009/m01`); a herd of wild cattle or camels (`quranic:root_000532:B014/m01`); a dried tree (`quranic:root_000217:B008/m01`); dry pasture broken by livestock or abandoned by the herder (`quranic:root_001578:B010/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:8-9,19-20 `رَبِّكَ/رَّبُّ/رَبِّهِ/رَبَّكَ`; 73:10 `ٱهْجُرْهُمْ`; 73:11 `ٱلنَّعْمَةِ`; 73:14 `ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:20 `ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ`
- Synthesis: Wind, green plant, forage, herd, drying, breakage, and abandonment create a full pasture cycle. It materializes bounty as an ecology vulnerable to exhaustion and usefully pressures the earth-and-mountain convulsion as the destruction of provision over time, not only sudden geology.

### S20. Whole Surah - Shaped Timber Eroded by Termites
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: Timber is cut into a supporting frame, dries in service, and is consumed from within by termites until its load-bearing integrity is lost.
- Active motifs: wooden tent supports or a carrying frame (`quranic:root_000067:B008/m01`); an adze for shaping wood (`quranic:root_001207:B008/m01`); dried timber (`quranic:root_000217:B008/m01`); termites that eat wood (`quranic:root_000025:B010/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:11 `أُو۟لِى`; 73:14,20 `ٱلْأَرْضُ/أَرْضِ/ٱلْجِبَالُ`; 73:20 `تُقَدِّمُوا`
- Synthesis: Tool, shaped support, drying material, hidden consumer, and structural loss form a compact failure process. It usefully reframes the surah’s collapsing supports: apparent solidity may already be hollowed internally before visible rupture or pouring begins.

### S21. Whole Surah - Counting Remembrance With Beads
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A worshiper names and remembers the divine referent while moving beads to count repeated utterances.
- Active motifs: beads used to count glorification (`quranic:root_000666:B006/m01`); verbal mention (`quranic:root_000516:B004/m01`); name as designation (`quranic:root_000745:B005/m01`); worship and the worshiped one (`quranic:root_000047:B001/m01`); exhaustive counting (`quranic:root_000332:B002/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:7 `سَبْحًا`; 73:8 `ٱذْكُرِ/ٱسْمَ`; 73:9,20 `إِلَٰهَ/ٱللَّهِ`; 73:20 `تُحْصُوهُ`
- Synthesis: Worshiper, named referent, repeated mention, counting instrument, and tally form a complete devotional practice. It directly materializes “remember the name” and presses the later admission that the night cannot be exhaustively counted: a finite instrument can order repetitions without mastering divine temporal measure.

### S22. Whole Surah - Pebble-in-Hand Odd-or-Even Game
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: One player hides pebbles in a closed hand, another calls odd or even, and opening and counting the hand resolves the guess.
- Active motifs: small pebbles used as counters (`quranic:root_000332:B001/m01`); an object concealed in the palm (`quranic:root_000637:B005/m01`); the game-call asking pair or odd (`quranic:root_000637:B005/m02`); resolution into an even pair or an odd count (`quranic:root_000637:B005/m03`); exhaustive enumeration (`quranic:root_000332:B002/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:20 `تُحْصُوهُ/ٱلزَّكَوٰةَ`
- Synthesis: Counters, concealment, binary call, exposure, and enumeration supply the full mechanics of a hand game. It usefully reframes the surah’s fractions and failed counting through a tactile contrast: hidden quantity can invite estimation, but only disclosure and enumeration settle the amount.

### S23. Whole Surah - Saddle and Tack Assembly
- Reading type: latent/lexical
- Scene or process: A wooden saddle frame is fitted with twisted cord and hanging straps, trimmed with a leatherworker’s blade, and balanced for rider and pillion.
- Active motifs: the wooden frame of a saddle (`quranic:root_001029:B009/m01`); rope twisted from three strands (`quranic:root_000203:B004/m01`); a hanging leather strap or cord (`quranic:root_000994:B006/m01`); a shoemaker’s or leatherworker’s blade (`quranic:root_000644:B008/m01`); pillion and balanced riding (`quranic:root_000644:B002/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:1 `ٱلْمُزَّمِّلُ`; 73:3,20 `ثُلُثَىِ/ثُلُثَهُ`; 73:13 `عَذَابًا`; 73:20 `أَعْظَمَ`
- Synthesis: Frame, cord, strap, cutting tool, rider, and pillion form a complete tack-making and load-balancing scene. It materializes the wrapped load-bearer and long travel by showing the crafted apparatus that distributes weight, while the three-strand cord gives numerical partition a structural function.

### S24. Whole Surah - Cutting Honor With Speech
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A sharp-tongued speaker rebukes, insults, backbites, and repeatedly diminishes another person’s social standing.
- Active motifs: a cutting or biting tongue (`quranic:root_000332:B004/m01`); backbiting and false accusation that cut another’s honor (`quranic:root_001217:B006/m01`); disparagement and verbal detraction (`quranic:root_001542:B003/m01`); harsh verbal rebuke (`quranic:root_001559:B004/m01`); an abusive sexualized slur (`quranic:root_001638:B004/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:3 `ٱنقُصْ`; 73:7,20 `ٱلنَّهَارِ`; 73:11 `وَذَرْنِى`; 73:20 `تُحْصُوهُ/قَرْضًا`
- Synthesis: Tongue, rebuke, slur, backbiting, and cumulative loss of standing constitute a specific social-injury process. It materializes “what they say” as repeated cutting of honor and thereby strengthens the force of beautiful withdrawal as refusal to answer injury with a matching verbal wound.

### S25. Whole Surah - Early Departure to a Sunlit Prayer Ground
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: A worshiper departs early, reaches an exposed place associated with sunrise or festival prayer, invokes God, and performs the prescribed prayer.
- Active motifs: going early to Friday prayer or another appointed gathering (`quranic:root_001578:B006/m01`); a sunlit sitting place or festival prayer ground (`quranic:root_000790:B006/m01`); prescribed prayer (`quranic:root_000879:B003/m01`); invoking the name of God (`quranic:root_000047:B002/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:9 `ٱلْمَشْرِقِ/إِلَٰهَ`; 73:10 `ٱهْجُرْهُمْ`; 73:20 `ٱللَّهِ/ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ`
- Synthesis: Early departure, timed place, invocation, and prayer form a complete public ritual itinerary. It supports the surah’s movement from measured night practice into maintained prayer by materializing punctual devotion as travel toward a light-defined communal site.

### S26. Whole Surah - Caravan Watering in Midday Heat
- Reading type: mixed
- Scene or process: Travelers reach a large basin during severe midday heat, drink life-sustaining water in measured breaths, and water their camels.
- Active motifs: road users and travelers (`quranic:root_000672:B002/m01`); severe midday heat and travel through it (`quranic:root_001578:B005/m01`); a large built water basin (`quranic:root_001578:B011/m01`); water that sustains life and restores breath (`quranic:root_001533:B008/m01`); the sound of camel lips taking water (`quranic:root_000833:B004/m01`); a sip or breath of drinking (`quranic:root_001533:B006/m01`)
- Ayah anchors: 73:10 `ٱهْجُرْهُمْ`; 73:17 `شِيبًا`; 73:19-20 `سَبِيلًا/سَبِيلِ`; 73:20 `أَنفُسِكُم`
- Synthesis: Heat, road party, basin, reviving water, measured human intake, and camel drinking complete a caravan-watering scene. It materializes the surah’s livelihood travel and merciful accommodation as bodily preservation, while reversing the choking meal with water that restores rather than obstructs breath.

## Cross-Pericope Parent Channels

### 38. Measured Demand Becomes Sustainable Communal Practice
- Semantic invariant: A measured obligation remains binding while its quantity and mode are adjusted to the real capacity of those performing it.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) prescribes and calibrates the night regimen; P2 (73:11-20) reports its communal performance, acknowledges failed exhaustive reckoning, and opens an easier textual measure. The bridge is a causal role progression with corrective contrast: prescription -> embodied attempt -> recognized limit -> accommodated continuation.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 1.A “Partitioning the night” and 1.B “Calibrating weight and correspondence”; P2 remains in 22.A “Portioning and counting the night,” 23.B “Constriction opening into relief,” and 24.A “Text gathered, learned, and made accessible.”
- Surface relation: direct; P1 73:2-6 supplies `قُمِ`, `قَلِيلًا`, `نِّصْفَهُ`, `ٱنقُصْ`, `زِدْ`, and `رَتِّلِ`; P2 73:20 supplies `تَقُومُ`, `ثُلُثَىِ`, `نِصْفَهُ`, `ثُلُثَهُ`, `يُقَدِّرُ`, `لَّن تُحْصُوهُ`, and `مَا تَيَسَّرَ`.
- Surprising reach: The closing concession does not cancel the opening formation; it completes its measurement logic by treating human limitation as evidence relevant to the measure itself.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 establishes a regimen through fraction, addition, subtraction, and ordered recitation. P2 shows that regimen embodied by a group, then distinguishes divine measure from human enumeration and preserves the practice through accessible reading. The whole-surah progression makes accommodation an act of accurate calibration rather than retreat from rigor.

### 39. The Cast Saying Advances Into Witnessed Event
- Semantic invariant: Communication becomes accountable as it moves from formed utterance, to delivered message, to witnessed reception, and finally to enacted truth.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) assembles, casts, and receives the weighty saying; P2 (73:11-20) dispatches an envoy, establishes testimony, and verifies a promise through occurrence. The bridge is a causal communication sequence and role progression: utterance -> transmission -> witness -> enactment.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 1.C “Assembling and transmitting ordered speech” and 2.B “Casting and receiving the weighty saying”; P2 remains in 17.A “Envoy, carried message, and witness” and 17.C “Commitment verified by action.”
- Surface relation: direct; P1 73:4-6 supplies `ٱلْقُرْءَانَ`, `نُلْقِى`, `قَوْلًا ثَقِيلًا`, and `قِيلًا`; P2 73:15-18 supplies `أَرْسَلْنَا`, `رَسُولًا شَٰهِدًا`, `عَصَىٰ`, and `كَانَ وَعْدُهُ مَفْعُولًا`.
- Surprising reach: The message does not merely recur in P2; it changes evidentiary role, moving from material borne by a recipient to the standard by which response and event are established.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 focuses on the formation and transfer of speech. P2 places that transfer inside an accountable sender-envoy-recipient relation and then lets the promised event confirm the message. The bridge supports the surah’s primary warning by making fulfilled judgment the terminal phase of communication, not a separate topic appended to it.

### 40. Human Release Transfers the Affair to Divine Custody
- Semantic invariant: Severance becomes viable when the released affair passes into a custody capable of sustaining responsibility and completing its outcome.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) directs severance toward the Lord and installs Him as guardian; P2 (73:11-20) explicitly transfers the deniers’ affair through `وَذَرْنِى` and distinguishes accountable custody from recoil, neglect, or disavowal. The bridge is causal role progression: release -> delegation -> sovereign handling.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 3.A “Severance directed toward an endpoint,” 3.C “Entrusting the released affair,” and 3.D “Failed delegation and exhausted dependence”; P2 remains in 36.A “Leaving an affair to accountable custody” and 36.B “Recoil, restraint, disavowal, and submission.”
- Surface relation: direct; P1 73:8-10 supplies `تَبَتَّلْ إِلَيْهِ`, `فَٱتَّخِذْهُ وَكِيلًا`, and `ٱهْجُرْهُمْ`; P2 73:11 supplies `وَذَرْنِى وَٱلْمُكَذِّبِينَ`, while 73:19 returns to `ٱتَّخَذَ إِلَىٰ رَبِّهِ سَبِيلًا`.
- Surprising reach: The abrupt shift from “leave them” to “leave Me with them” specifies the new holder of the relation and prevents withdrawal from being read as unowned abandonment.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 removes the addressee from direct entanglement and redirects reliance toward a sufficient guardian. P2 names the custody transfer and carries it into accountability. The bridge therefore turns devotional withdrawal into a change of responsible agent: the human releases precisely because the sovereign does not.

### 41. Ethical Restraint and Bounded Reprieve Share a Form but Not an Outcome
- Semantic invariant: Withholding immediate reaction creates a temporal interval whose meaning depends on what the interval is for.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) withholds retaliatory engagement through patience and beautiful departure; P2 (73:11-20) withholds punishment through a short reprieve that remains bounded by fulfilled promise. The bridge is a shared restraint invariant sharpened by contrast: ethical non-retaliation preserves conduct, while reprieve delays but does not remove consequence.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 3.B “Beautiful non-engagement”; P2 remains in 14.A “A short reprieve before an appointed time” and 14.B “Promise becoming an accomplished event.”
- Surface relation: direct; P1 73:10 supplies `ٱصْبِرْ` and `ٱهْجُرْهُمْ هَجْرًا جَمِيلًا`; P2 73:11 supplies `مَهِّلْهُمْ قَلِيلًا`, and 73:18 closes the interval with `كَانَ وَعْدُهُ مَفْعُولًا`.
- Surprising reach: Patience and delay are not collapsed into passivity: the same withholding form protects moral proportion in one pericope and preserves the certainty of timed judgment in the other.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 suspends reciprocal injury so departure can remain beautiful. P2 suspends arrival only long enough to establish reprieve as measured rather than indefinite. The contrast clarifies that restraint can govern the manner of action without negating its endpoint.

### 42. Carried Formation Reverses Into Inescapable Closure
- Semantic invariant: Pressure either becomes a voluntarily stabilized burden or an externally imposed condition that closes movement and intake.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) presents the wrapped addressee as a carrier who rises under a weight; P2 (73:11-20) turns pressure outward as fetter, capture, throat obstruction, and surrounding fire. The bridge is a contrast and reversal in bodily agency: assumed load -> stabilized carriage versus imposed load -> immobilization.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 2.A “The wrapped load-bearer”; P2 remains in 15.A “Binding, capture, and the neck grip,” 15.B “Food that cannot be swallowed,” and 15.C “Enclosure by fire and molten heat.”
- Surface relation: direct; P1 73:1-5 supplies `ٱلْمُزَّمِّلُ`, `قُمِ`, and `قَوْلًا ثَقِيلًا`; P2 73:12-13 supplies `أَنكَالًا`, `جَحِيمًا`, `طَعَامًا ذَا غُصَّةٍ`, and `عَذَابًا أَلِيمًا`.
- Surprising reach: The surah distributes heaviness according to response: formative weight can be borne and integrated, whereas rejected address returns as an environment that prevents ordinary bodily passage.
- Bridge synthesis: P1’s carrier accepts pressure by rising and balancing beneath it. P2 removes that agency: limbs, throat, and surroundings become sites of closure. The reversal usefully pressures the “heavy saying” as a load whose willing reception forms capacity before weight arrives in a punitive form that cannot be carried.

### 43. Controlled Waterwork Reverses Into Boundary Failure
- Semantic invariant: Flow remains productive only while source, channel, vessel, boundary, and outlet keep their assigned roles.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) assembles a managed hydraulic scene of drawing, receiving, channeling, and loaded conveyance; P2 (73:11-20) supplies runoff, flood, and the failure of containment, while retaining managed flow as the lost baseline. The bridge is a repeated scene signature with causal reversal: capture and direction -> excess -> breached boundary.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 6.A “Well, bucket, trough, and reservoir,” 6.B “Opening and directing a channel,” and 6.C “A loaded vessel under a captain”; P2 remains in 16.C “Water exceeding channels and boundaries” and 16.D “Managed flow as the failed baseline.”
- Surface relation: indirect; P1 73:5-9 supplies the activating roots in `نُلْقِى`, `سَبْحًا`, `رَبِّكَ`, and `ٱلْمَغْرِبِ`; P2 73:14,18-20 supplies `ٱلْأَرْضُ`, `ٱلسَّمَآءُ مُنفَطِرٌ`, `سَبِيلًا`, and `فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ`.
- Surprising reach: Cosmic instability is reframed through ordinary water management: disaster is not water as a topic but the loss of differentiated hydraulic functions.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 makes water usable by assigning each component a bounded operation. P2 preserves the same architecture only to show runoff crossing it and enclosure failing. The bridge materializes judgment as systemic role collapse: source, route, land, and boundary cease cooperating in a controlled passage.

### 44. The Recurring Horizon Clock Narrows Into an Appointment
- Semantic invariant: Observable position makes time legible, but recurring cycles can also point toward an event that will not recur as ordinary day and night do.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) reads night, daylight, east, west, sun height, and weather as a recurring clock; P2 (73:11-20) reads lunar stations, named days, and first-later positions as markers of a promised appointment. The bridge is a repeated timekeeping scene with scalar progression: daily cycle -> calendar position -> fixed event.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 5.A “The horizon as a clock” and 5.C “Sky movement and layered weather”; P2 remains in 14.C “Reading the celestial calendar” and 14.D “First, later, forward, and rear positions.”
- Surface relation: direct; P1 73:2,7,9 supplies `ٱلَّيْلَ`, `ٱلنَّهَارِ`, `ٱلْمَشْرِقِ`, and `ٱلْمَغْرِبِ`; P2 73:14,17-18 supplies `يَوْمَ`, `يَوْمًا`, and `وَعْدُهُ`.
- Surprising reach: The final day is not linked to ordinary time by topic alone; both pericopes use position, interval, and visible marker to make an otherwise absent time operationally knowable.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 trains attention on a cycle whose phases return. P2 keeps the same observational grammar but directs it toward succession and appointment. The progression reframes the promised event as temporally ordered and legible even though its arrival terminates ordinary repetition.

### 45. Daytime Roaming Divides Into Livelihood and Combat Routes
- Semantic invariant: Open daytime movement acquires moral and social definition from the purpose pursued along its route.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) supplies broad daytime roaming and travel; P2 (73:11-20) divides that mobility into seeking divine bounty and fighting in God’s path. The bridge is a repeated traversal signature with role differentiation: available movement -> commercial journey or military advance.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 5.B “Riding through night and roaming by day”; P2 remains in 21.B “Commercial travel in search of surplus” and 21.C “Advancing along a battle route.”
- Surface relation: direct; P1 73:7 supplies `فِى ٱلنَّهَارِ سَبْحًا طَوِيلًا`; P2 73:20 supplies `يَضْرِبُونَ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ يَبْتَغُونَ مِن فَضْلِ ٱللَّهِ` and `يُقَٰتِلُونَ فِى سَبِيلِ ٱللَّهِ`.
- Surprising reach: P2 answers what the long daytime “swim” contains by assigning its travelers different objectives, risks, and forms of return.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 establishes daylight as a wide field of motion without reducing it to one task. P2 differentiates the agents occupying that field and names the ends that govern their routes. The bridge turns temporal availability into accountable purpose: the same land supports distinct journeys because intention and destination assign the movement its role.

### 46. Ordinary Maturation Is Compressed Into Catastrophic Aging
- Semantic invariant: Life normally advances through reproductive preparation, gestation, birth, upbringing, and visible maturation; catastrophe collapses those separated stages.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) builds the ordinary generative sequence from coupling through carried pregnancy to mature strength; P2 (73:11-20) supplies delivery, kinship, recurrent reproductive time, and the immediate conversion of children into grey-haired figures. The bridge is a repeated life-cycle signature with temporal reversal: extended development -> instantaneous terminal marker.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 8.A “Coupling and conception,” 8.B “Womb, pregnancy, and carried weight,” and 8.C “Upbringing into mature strength”; P2 remains in 19.A “Womb, delivery, and afterbirth,” 19.B “Childhood turning immediately to old age,” 19.C “Emergence, growth, and bodily markers,” 19.D “Lineage, household, and age cohort,” and 19.E “Reproductive interval and recurrence.”
- Surface relation: indirect in P1 and direct in P2; P1 73:5-6 supplies `ثَقِيلًا` and `نَاشِئَةَ`, while P2 73:17 supplies `يَجْعَلُ ٱلْوِلْدَٰنَ شِيبًا`.
- Surprising reach: P2’s child-to-grey image becomes catastrophic precisely because P1 supplies the distributed biological and social duration that the image deletes.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 establishes life as accumulated formation carried by bodies and caregivers. P2 repeats the reproductive architecture, then forces its beginning and late marker together. The cross-pericope reversal makes the promised day an assault on duration itself: it does not merely age the young but abolishes the interval by which growth is ordinarily made intelligible.

### 47. Inward Saying Is Tested by Public Denial and Assent
- Semantic invariant: Speech becomes accountable when inward appropriation, public utterance, and durable response either correspond or diverge.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) moves saying from inward representation through articulation, rumor, injury, and conviction; P2 (73:11-20) tests the message through denial, deceptive representation, verbal assent, bodily submission, and unstable alignment. The bridge is a repeated discourse signature with contrast and role progression: conceived speech -> circulated claim -> recipient response -> tested allegiance.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 4.A “From inner saying to articulated sign,” 4.B “Rumor, fabrication, and verbal injury,” and 4.D “Appropriating a saying as conviction”; P2 remains in 17.B “Denial, rumor, and deceptive representation” and 17.D “Answer, assent, and unstable alignment.”
- Surface relation: direct; P1 73:5-6,10 supplies `قَوْلًا`, `قِيلًا`, and `مَا يَقُولُونَ`; P2 73:11,16-17 supplies `ٱلْمُكَذِّبِينَ`, `عَصَىٰ ... ٱلرَّسُولَ`, and `كَفَرْتُمْ`.
- Surprising reach: The bridge distinguishes opposition from a second failure mode: words and gestures may appear compliant while allegiance remains opportunistic.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 establishes that speech can begin inwardly, circulate falsely, or settle as conviction. P2 assigns those possibilities to recipients whose denial, answer, posture, and durable position can be compared. The whole-surah test is therefore correspondence across layers, not verbal formula alone.

### 48. Named Remembrance Becomes Maintained Service and Care
- Semantic invariant: Devotion persists when remembered designation, embodied practice, sustaining authority, and outward transfer remain joined.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) names, remembers, stands, reads, worships, and entrusts care to a sovereign guardian; P2 (73:11-20) maintains prayer, purifies value through giving, and extends sustaining rule into accountable service. The bridge is causal role progression: remembered name -> embodied worship -> maintained institution -> care-bearing transfer.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 4.C “Name, reminder, and reputation,” 13.A “Worship through standing, reading, and remembrance,” and 13.B “Sovereign, teacher, caretaker, and guarantor”; P2 remains in 24.B “Standing and maintaining prayer,” 24.C “Purifying transfer,” and 28.A “Household rule and sustaining authority.”
- Surface relation: direct; P1 73:2,4,8-9 supplies `قُمِ`, `ٱلْقُرْءَانَ`, `ٱذْكُرِ ٱسْمَ رَبِّكَ`, and `وَكِيلًا`; P2 73:20 supplies `أَقِيمُوا ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ`, `ءَاتُوا ٱلزَّكَوٰةَ`, `أَقْرِضُوا ٱللَّهَ`, and `رَّحِيمٌ`.
- Surprising reach: The closing acts are not a list added to private remembrance; they are its institutional and interpersonal continuation.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 forms the worshiper through named attention, posture, reading, and reliable guardianship. P2 gives that formation durable public operations: prayer is maintained, value is purified by transfer, and authority sustains dependents. The progression supports devotion as care that outlives the initiating moment of remembrance.

### 49. Stored Value Advances, Circulates, and Returns Enlarged
- Semantic invariant: Value is assessed and preserved so it can move through livelihood, contribution, and reciprocal return without losing its accountable measure.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) supplies weighing, pricing, bargaining, travel stores, concentration, and preservation; P2 (73:11-20) supplies loan, capital, advancing a deed, finding enlarged reward, and generous distribution. The bridge is a causal economic sequence: valuation -> storage -> productive movement -> transfer -> return.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 9.A “Weighing, pricing, and bargaining,” 9.B “Means, livelihood, and travel stores,” 10.A “Reducing and concentrating food,” and 10.B “Packing and serving durable stores”; P2 remains in 22.B “Loan, capital, and reciprocal return,” 22.C “Advancing a deed and finding it enlarged,” and 25.C “Food, livelihood, and generous distribution.”
- Surface relation: indirect in P1 and direct in P2; P1 73:2-7 supplies the quantity, weight, increase, and travel activators in `قَلِيلًا`, `نِّصْفَهُ`, `زِدْ`, `ثَقِيلًا`, and `سَبْحًا`; P2 73:20 supplies `فَضْلِ ٱللَّهِ`, `ءَاتُوا`, `أَقْرِضُوا`, `تُقَدِّمُوا`, `تَجِدُوهُ`, and `أَعْظَمَ أَجْرًا`.
- Surprising reach: Preservation and circulation are joined by operation rather than a broad “provision” topic: what is measured and stored becomes capable of travel, gift, advance, and accountable return.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 builds the material preconditions for durable value. P2 assigns direction and ethics to its movement, distinguishing capital, loan, gift, and reward. The sequence reframes good deeds as advances that persist through transfer and return, while generosity prevents the value system from closing around private accumulation.

### 50. Ethical Proportion Reverses the Value of Luxury
- Semantic invariant: Visible quality receives its true value from proportionate conduct rather than rank, comfort, or enlarged display.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) joins bodily proportion and beauty to fair, restrained conduct; P2 (73:11-20) presents affluent prestige, display, and arrogant enlargement before dispersal and abasement. The bridge is a shared valuation invariant with contrast and reversal: measured beauty -> ethical grace versus possessed luxury -> status collapse.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 7.A “A well-proportioned body and face” and 7.B “Grace as proportionate conduct”; P2 remains in 27.A “Rank, comfort, and recognized excellence,” 27.B “Display, fame, and arrogant enlargement,” and 27.C “Dispersal and abasement.”
- Surface relation: indirect but strongly anchored; P1 73:3,6,10 supplies `نِّصْفَهُ`, `أَقْوَمُ`, and `هَجْرًا جَمِيلًا`; P2 73:11 supplies `أُو۟لِى ٱلنَّعْمَةِ`, followed by the punitive reversal of 73:12-14.
- Surprising reach: Beauty and luxury are not parallel positive topics. The pericopes reverse their valuation by testing whether visible excellence is governed by proportion or detached from it.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 makes grace an achieved relation among measure, body, restraint, and conduct. P2 isolates prestige from that ethical architecture and lets display expand until the collective loses standing. The reversal supports beautiful withdrawal as a stronger form of value than affluent visibility.

### 51. Constructed Covering Reverses Into Exposure
- Semantic invariant: A cover protects only while material, boundary, and moral function remain intact; rupture changes the covered interior into an exposed object.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) supplies a treated garment, veil, and wrapped wearer; P2 (73:11-20) scales covering into barrier, forgiveness, roof, and sky, then stages concealment and rupture. The bridge is a repeated covering signature with contrast and reversal: fabricated protection -> moral concealment or shielding -> failed canopy -> exposure.
- Canonical scene placements: The P1 garment scene remains in S2 “Treated Garment and Veil”; P2 remains in 18.A “Protective barriers,” 18.B “Concealing truth and covering fault,” and 18.C “Roof, drape, and sky torn open.”
- Surface relation: indirect in P1 and direct in P2; P1 73:1,3,7-9 supplies `ٱلْمُزَّمِّلُ`, `نِّصْفَهُ`, `سَبْحًا`, and `رَبِّكَ`; P2 73:17-20 supplies `تَتَّقُونَ`, `كَفَرْتُمْ`, `ٱلسَّمَآءُ مُنفَطِرٌ`, and `غَفُورٌ`.
- Surprising reach: The wrapped figure and ruptured sky occupy opposite ends of one boundary operation, while disbelief and forgiveness show that concealment can either corrupt or protect.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 begins with a body enclosed by a worked covering. P2 expands enclosure to architecture and cosmos, then separates harmful concealment from protective covering before tearing the upper boundary open. The reversal materializes exposure as the loss of an achieved protective relation, not mere visibility.

### 52. Bodily Distress Advances From Symptom to Accommodation
- Semantic invariant: Involuntary bodily signs become ethically relevant when they are recognized as impairment, placed under care, and allowed to modify demanded action.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) supplies acute facial, sensory, tremor, pain, and movement failures; P2 (73:11-20) develops visible crisis signs, names illness, tends its progression, and adjusts capacity to what is viable. The bridge is a causal clinical role progression: symptom -> recognized condition -> care -> accommodated performance.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 12.A “Face, ear, and eye under impairment” and 12.B “Tremor, pain, and burdened movement”; P2 remains in 37.A “Convulsion and heated anger,” 37.B “Face, eye, lip, and hair as diagnostic surface,” 23.A “Illness progressing under care,” and 20.C “Capacity adjusted to ease and suitability.”
- Surface relation: indirect in P1 and direct in P2; P1 73:5-6 supplies `ثَقِيلًا`, `أَشَدُّ`, and `وَطْـًٔا`; P2 73:14,17,20 supplies `تَرْجُفُ`, `شِيبًا`, `مَرْضَىٰ`, and `مَا تَيَسَّرَ`.
- Surprising reach: The closing accommodation is grounded in a chain of bodily evidence rather than generalized leniency.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 gives impairment a sensorimotor vocabulary but does not yet name the accommodated subject. P2 makes symptoms visible, recognizes illness as a changing condition, and recalibrates action. The progression supports mercy as accurate response to embodied limitation and blocks any equation of reduced capacity with refusal.

### 53. Interpersonal Grappling Scales Into Organized Capture
- Semantic invariant: Conflict advances from direct bodily contest through coordinated engagement to capture, subjugation, and mortal outcome.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) supplies fastening, immobilization, grappling, crushing, and feud; P2 (73:11-20) scales the same interaction into mobilization, reciprocal combat, capture, death exposure, and coercive subjugation. The bridge is a repeated contest signature with role and scale progression: individual hold -> opposing sides -> organized force -> captured body.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 11.A “Fastening and immobilizing a body” and 11.B “Grappling, crushing, and feud”; P2 remains in 29.A “Mobilization and engagement,” 29.B “Capture and exposure to death,” and 29.C “Subjugation and the heavy instrument.”
- Surface relation: indirect in P1 and direct in P2; P1 73:5-6,9-10 supplies `ثَقِيلًا`, `أَشَدُّ`, `فَٱتَّخِذْهُ`, and `ٱصْبِرْ`; P2 73:12,16,20 supplies `أَنكَالًا`, `أَخَذْنَٰهُ`, `يَضْرِبُونَ`, and `يُقَٰتِلُونَ`.
- Surprising reach: War is not joined to grappling by violence as a topic; both scenes preserve the same roles of opposing agent, controlled limb, applied force, lost mobility, and terminal custody.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 defines conflict at body scale, where holds and fastening determine who can move. P2 adds scouting, sides, engagement, and post-capture force without changing that core mechanics. The scale progression materializes organized conflict as coordinated control over bodies rather than an abstract collective event.

### 54. Patterned Recitation Is Contrasted With Fabricated Composition
- Semantic invariant: Verbal form can follow a disciplined model that stabilizes recurrence or manufacture an artifact without truthful correspondence.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) supplies poetic model and repeated rhyme as formal ordering; P2 (73:11-20) supplies poetic composition, representation, replicable form, and fabricated discourse. The bridge is a shared formal-construction invariant sharpened by contrast: patterned recurrence -> accountable form versus formal making -> possible fabrication.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in S1 “Verse Modeling and Repeated Rhyme”; P2 remains in S5 “Formal Text Modeling and Composition.”
- Surface relation: indirect; P1 73:4,6 supplies `ٱلْقُرْءَانَ`, `تَرْتِيلًا`, and `وَطْـًٔا`; P2 73:18,20 supplies `مَفْعُولًا`, `ٱقْرَءُوا`, `ٱلْقُرْءَانِ`, `قَرْضًا`, and `يَضْرِبُونَ`.
- Surprising reach: Formal resemblance is not treated as proof of truth; the same capacity to model and compose can support disciplined recitation or produce a persuasive counterfeit.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 emphasizes recurrence governed by a prior pattern and stable rhyme. P2 broadens formal making into representation and composition, then exposes fabrication as its truth-failing possibility. The contrast supports ordered recitation by locating its value in correspondence and accountable transmission, not patterned form alone.

### 55. Firm Night Footing Reverses Into Cosmic Loss of Support
- Semantic invariant: Stability depends on pressure finding a resistant support and boundaries remaining intact; judgment removes both conditions.
- Pericopes and bridge: P1 (73:1-10) turns strong night pressure into congruent footing and upright stability; P2 (73:11-20) makes earth and mountains shake, solid mass pour away, and the overhead boundary split. The bridge is a structural contrast and reversal: pressure -> stable contact versus oscillation and rupture -> loss of support.
- Canonical scene placements: P1 remains in 2.C “Foot pressure becoming firm contact”; P2 remains in 16.A “Mountain mass becoming poured sand” and 16.B “The overhead cover splitting open.”
- Surface relation: direct; P1 73:6 supplies `أَشَدُّ وَطْـًٔا وَأَقْوَمُ`; P2 73:14 supplies `تَرْجُفُ ٱلْأَرْضُ وَٱلْجِبَالُ` and `كَثِيبًا مَّهِيلًا`, while 73:18 supplies `ٱلسَّمَآءُ مُنفَطِرٌ`.
- Surprising reach: Uprightness and cosmic collapse are connected by mechanics rather than opposition as a loose theme: one scene stabilizes contact under force, while the other removes resistance below and enclosure above.
- Bridge synthesis: P1 shows pressure converted into formation because foot and ground correspond. P2 breaks that relation at every scale: ground oscillates, mountain aggregate flows, and sky enclosure opens. The reversal makes night formation a local achievement of stability inside a surah whose judgment scene abolishes the supports on which ordinary stability depends.
